Read the status with the finding. Findings include established vulnerabilities and poor practice to remediate or refactor. An exploit demonstration is not required for a poor-practice finding. Review means the evidence does not yet establish a problem or adequate protection. Protection applies only to the stated claim. No findings reported does not mean a route is safe. Counts are per use and route; shared helpers may appear more than once.

Remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.
Illustrative remediation example · unverified
// Illustrative Sequelize binding pattern; adapt schema and full authentication logic.
const rows = await sequelize.query(
  'SELECT id FROM accounts WHERE email = $email',
  { bind: { email: req.body.email } }
);

server/routes.py:122

def dashboard():
    # Check if the user is logged in
    if 'username' in session:

        search_query = request.args.get('search', '')

        # Get user_id from the session username
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
            cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
            user = cursor.fetchone()
            user_id = user['id']

        if search_query:
            s_query = f"SELECT filename FROM files WHERE user_id = {user_id} AND filename LIKE '%{search_query}%'"
            cursor.execute(s_query)
        else:
            cursor.execute("SELECT filename FROM files WHERE user_id = ?", (user_id,))
        files = [row['filename'] for row in cursor.fetchall()]

        return render_template('dashboard.html', files=files, search_query=search_query)
    else:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

Remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Use escaped template output for HTML text and quoted non-active attributes; use textContent for plain DOM text.
  • For intentional rich HTML, apply a verified maintained sanitizer before every sink; inspect its configuration and version.
  • Treat script, URL, event-handler and unquoted-attribute contexts separately. HTML entity encoding alone does not protect them.
  • Preserve source, storage and output links when fixing reflected or stored flows; test the protected counterpart.

def dashboard():
    # Check if the user is logged in
    if 'username' in session:

        search_query = request.args.get('search', '')

        # Get user_id from the session username
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
            cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
            user = cursor.fetchone()
            user_id = user['id']

        if search_query:
            s_query = f"SELECT filename FROM files WHERE user_id = {user_id} AND filename LIKE '%{search_query}%'"
            cursor.execute(s_query)
        else:
            cursor.execute("SELECT filename FROM files WHERE user_id = ?", (user_id,))
        files = [row['filename'] for row in cursor.fetchall()]

        return render_template('dashboard.html', files=files, search_query=search_query)
    else:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

                            </form>

                        </div>
                    </p>
                    <ul id="comments-list"></ul>
                    <hr>
                </li>
                {% endfor %}
            </ul>
        </section>

        <section>
            <div class="search-container">

    <script src="https://code.jquery.com/jquery-3.6.0.min.js"></script>
    <script>
        $(document).ready(function() {
            // Show flash messages if present
            $('#flash-message .flash').each(function() {
                $(this).fadeIn().delay(3000).fadeOut();
            });

            // Handle file deletion
            $('.delete-btn').click(function(event) {
                event.preventDefault();

                var filename = $(this).data('filename');
                var $fileItem = $(this).closest('.file-item');

                $.ajax({
                    type: 'POST',
                    url: '/delete/' + filename,
                    success: function(response) {
                        $('#flash-message').text(response.message).removeClass('success error').addClass(response.type).fadeIn().delay(3000).fadeOut();
                        if (response.type === 'success') {
                            $fileItem.remove();
                        }
                    },
                    error: function(xhr, status, error) {
                        $('#flash-message').text('An error occurred while deleting the file.').removeClass('success').addClass('error').fadeIn().delay(3000).fadeOut();
                        console.error('Error:', error);
                    }
                });
            });

            // Handle comment section toggle
            $('.comment-btn').click(function() {
                var $commentSection = $(this).closest('.file-item').find('.comment-section');
                $commentSection.slideToggle();
            });

            // Handle comment submission
            $('.file-item').each(function() {
                var $fileItem = $(this);

                $fileItem.find('.comment-section form').submit(function(event) {
                    event.preventDefault();

                    var comment = $fileItem.find('.comment-section input').val();
                    var $commentList = $fileItem.find('#comments-list');

                    if (comment) {
                        $commentList.append('<li>' + comment + '</li>');
                        $fileItem.find('.comment-section input').val('');
                    }
                });
            });
        });

    </script>
</body>
</html>

def dashboard():
    # Check if the user is logged in
    if 'username' in session:

        search_query = request.args.get('search', '')

        # Get user_id from the session username
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
            cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
            user = cursor.fetchone()
            user_id = user['id']

        if search_query:
            s_query = f"SELECT filename FROM files WHERE user_id = {user_id} AND filename LIKE '%{search_query}%'"
            cursor.execute(s_query)
        else:
            cursor.execute("SELECT filename FROM files WHERE user_id = ?", (user_id,))
        files = [row['filename'] for row in cursor.fetchall()]

        return render_template('dashboard.html', files=files, search_query=search_query)
    else:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

def get_user_details_from_database(user_id):
    # Query the database for the user using parameterized query
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (user_id,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
    return user

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

);

def get_db_connection():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DATABASE_URI)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row  # This allows name-based access to columns
    return conn

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Use escaped template output for HTML text and quoted non-active attributes; use textContent for plain DOM text.
  • For intentional rich HTML, apply a verified maintained sanitizer before every sink; inspect its configuration and version.
  • Treat script, URL, event-handler and unquoted-attribute contexts separately. HTML entity encoding alone does not protect them.
  • Preserve source, storage and output links when fixing reflected or stored flows; test the protected counterpart.

server/routes.py:148

def profile():
    # Check if the user is logged in
    if 'username' in session:
        user = get_user_details_from_database(session['user_id'])
        return render_template('profile.html', user=user)
    else:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

server/routes.py:406

def get_user_details_from_database(user_id):
    # Query the database for the user using parameterized query
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (user_id,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
    return user

client/templates/profile.html:1

<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
    <meta charset="UTF-8">
    <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0">
    <title>ThreatByteLand - User Profile</title>
    <link rel="stylesheet" href="{{ url_for('static', filename='css/styles.css') }}">
</head>
<body>
    <header>
        <h2>User Profile</h2>
        <nav>
            <ul>
                <li><a href="{{ url_for('dashboard') }}">Uploads Dashboard</a></li>
                <li><a href="{{ url_for('logout') }}">Log Out</a></li>
            </ul>
        </nav>
    </header>
    <main>
        <div id="flash-message">
            {% with messages = get_flashed_messages(with_categories=true) %}
                {% if messages %}
                    <div class="flash {{ messages[0][0] }}">{{ messages[0][1] }}</div>
                {% endif %}
            {% endwith %}
        </div>

        <section>
            <h3>Your Profile</h3>
            <div class="profile-info">
                <div class="profile-picture">
                    {% if user.profile_picture %}
                        <img src="{{ url_for('profile_pictures', filename=user.profile_picture | string + '.png') }}" alt="Profile Picture">
                    {% else %}
                        <img src="{{ url_for('profile_pictures', filename='default_profile_picture.png') }}" alt="Default Profile Picture">
                    {% endif %}
                </div>
                <div class="profile-details">
                    <p><strong>Username:</strong> {{ user.username }}</p>
                    <p><strong>Email:</strong> {{ user.email }}</p>
                    <p><strong>Country:</strong> {{ user.country | safe}}</p>
                    <p><strong>Join Date:</strong> {{ user.created_at }}</p>

                    <p><strong>Role:</strong> {{ user.role }}</p>
                    <p><strong>Permissions:</strong> {{ user.permissions }}</p>
                    <p><strong>Team:</strong> {{ user.team }}</p>
                    <p><strong>Last Login:</strong> {{ user.last_login }}</p>
                </div>
            </div>
            <a href="{{ url_for('edit_profile') }}" class="btn">Edit Profile</a>
        </section>
    </main>
    <footer>
        <p>&copy; 2024 ThreatByteLand</p>
    </footer>

    <script src="https://code.jquery.com/jquery-3.6.0.min.js"></script>
    <script>
        $(document).ready(function() {
            // Show flash messages if present
            $('#flash-message .flash').each(function() {
                $(this).fadeIn().delay(3000).fadeOut();
            });
        });
    </script>
</body>
</html>

def get_user_details_from_database(user_id):
    # Query the database for the user using parameterized query
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (user_id,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
    return user

Remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

server/api/v1/api_v1.py:178

def post(self):
        """
        Updates a user profile information.
        """
        print(session)
        if 'username' in session:
            user_id = session['user_id']
            data = request.json

            if 'user_id' in data and data['user_id'] != user_id:
                abort(403, description="Unauthorized to modify other user profiles.")

            allowed_updates = ['email', 'country', 'team']
            sensitive_updates = ['role', 'permissions']

            updates = {key: value for key, value in data.items() if key in allowed_updates}

            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                query = "SELECT username, email, country, role, permissions, team FROM users WHERE id = ?"
                cursor.execute(query, (user_id,))
                user_profile = cursor.fetchone()

            if user_profile['role'] == "admin":
                updates.update({key: value for key, value in data.items() if key in sensitive_updates})

            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                update_query = "UPDATE users SET " + ", ".join([f"{key} = ?" for key in updates.keys()]) + " WHERE id = ?"
                cursor.execute(update_query, list(updates.values()) + [user_id])
                conn.commit()

            return {"success": True, "message": "Profile updated successfully."}, 200
        else:
            abort(401, description="Authentication required.")

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Remove the value from the log entry, or log a non-revealing fact (presence, length, match result, or a non-reversible identifier) instead.
  • Log an allowlist of fields rather than whole request bodies, queries, or header sets. Never log Authorization or Cookie header values.
  • If redaction is required, apply a verified redaction before the logger call or in the logger's configuration, and test that emitted lines no longer contain the value.
  • Do not send credentials in URL query strings; access logs, proxies, and browser history record URLs. Use a request body or header instead.

Use use:route:server/api/v1/api_v1.py:9796:POST:/api/v1/update_picture:server/api/v1/api_v1.py:10320:outbound_http_candidate · outbound_http_candidate · Assessment completed openai gpt-6-luna

server/api/v1/api_v1.py:243

def post(self):
        """
        Update the user's profile picture by fetching it from a provided URL.
        This endpoint is vulnerable to SSRF, allowing external service interaction.
        """
        data = api.payload
        username = data['username']
        picture_url = data['picture_url']

        try:
            # Fetch the picture from the URL provided by the user
            response = requests.get(picture_url)

            # Check if the request was successful
            if response.status_code == 200:
                # Here, instead of actually saving the picture, we'll just simulate that process.
                # Vulnerability point: Fetching content from an arbitrary URL provided by the user
                if 'image/jpeg' in response.headers['Content-Type'] or 'image/png' in response.headers['Content-Type']:

                    conn = get_db_connection()
                    cursor = conn.cursor()

                    # Get the user by username
                    cursor.execute("SELECT id, profile_picture FROM users WHERE username = ?", (username,))
                    current_user = cursor.fetchone()

                    if current_user:
                        user_id = current_user['id']
                        profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']

                        # Generate a new profile picture ID if necessary
                        if profile_picture_id is None:
                            cursor.execute("SELECT MAX(profile_picture) FROM users")
                            max_id = cursor.fetchone()[0]
                            if max_id is None:
                                max_id = 0
                            profile_picture_id = max_id + 1

                        # Save the profile picture file
                        filename = str(profile_picture_id) + '.png'
                        file_path = os.path.join(Config.PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
                        with open(file_path, 'wb') as file:
                            file.write(response.content)

                        # Update the user's profile picture ID in the database
                        cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET profile_picture = ? WHERE id = ?", (profile_picture_id, user_id))
                        conn.commit()
                        return {'message': f'Profile picture updated successfully for user {username}'}, 200
                    else:
                        return {'error': 'User not found'}, 404
                else:
                    return {'error': 'Invalid image format'}, 400
            else:
                return {'message': 'Failed to fetch the picture from the provided URL'}, 400
        except Exception as e:
            return {'message': str(e)}, 500

                    current_user = cursor.fetchone()

                    if current_user:
                        user_id = current_user['id']
                        profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']

                        # Generate a new profile picture ID if necessary
                        if profile_picture_id is None:
                            cursor.execute("SELECT MAX(profile_picture) FROM users")
                            max_id = cursor.fetchone()[0]
                            if max_id is None:
                                max_id = 0
                            profile_picture_id = max_id + 1

                        # Save the profile picture file
                        filename = str(profile_picture_id) + '.png'
                        file_path = os.path.join(Config.PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
                        with open(file_path, 'wb') as file:
                            file.write(response.content)

                        # Update the user's profile picture ID in the database
                        cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET profile_picture = ? WHERE id = ?", (profile_picture_id, user_id))
                        conn.commit()
                        return {'message': f'Profile picture updated successfully for user {username}'}, 200
                    else:
                        return {'error': 'User not found'}, 404
                else:
                    return {'error': 'Invalid image format'}, 400
            else:
                return {'message': 'Failed to fetch the picture from the provided URL'}, 400
        except Exception as e:
            return {'message': str(e)}, 500

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

server/api/v1/api_v1.py:243

def post(self):
        """
        Update the user's profile picture by fetching it from a provided URL.
        This endpoint is vulnerable to SSRF, allowing external service interaction.
        """
        data = api.payload
        username = data['username']
        picture_url = data['picture_url']

        try:
            # Fetch the picture from the URL provided by the user
            response = requests.get(picture_url)

            # Check if the request was successful
            if response.status_code == 200:
                # Here, instead of actually saving the picture, we'll just simulate that process.
                # Vulnerability point: Fetching content from an arbitrary URL provided by the user
                if 'image/jpeg' in response.headers['Content-Type'] or 'image/png' in response.headers['Content-Type']:

                    conn = get_db_connection()
                    cursor = conn.cursor()

                    # Get the user by username
                    cursor.execute("SELECT id, profile_picture FROM users WHERE username = ?", (username,))
                    current_user = cursor.fetchone()

                    if current_user:
                        user_id = current_user['id']
                        profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']

                        # Generate a new profile picture ID if necessary
                        if profile_picture_id is None:
                            cursor.execute("SELECT MAX(profile_picture) FROM users")
                            max_id = cursor.fetchone()[0]
                            if max_id is None:
                                max_id = 0
                            profile_picture_id = max_id + 1

                        # Save the profile picture file
                        filename = str(profile_picture_id) + '.png'
                        file_path = os.path.join(Config.PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
                        with open(file_path, 'wb') as file:
                            file.write(response.content)

                        # Update the user's profile picture ID in the database
                        cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET profile_picture = ? WHERE id = ?", (profile_picture_id, user_id))
                        conn.commit()
                        return {'message': f'Profile picture updated successfully for user {username}'}, 200
                    else:
                        return {'error': 'User not found'}, 404
                else:
                    return {'error': 'Invalid image format'}, 400
            else:
                return {'message': 'Failed to fetch the picture from the provided URL'}, 400
        except Exception as e:
            return {'message': str(e)}, 500

server/routes.py:66

def signup():
    """
    Handles user registration.

    On GET request:
    Renders the signup page.

    On POST request:
    Retrieves user registration data from the signup form.
    Performs basic validation checks on the input data.
    Checks if the provided username already exists in the database.
    Inserts the new user into the database.
    Stores the username in the session upon successful registration and redirects to the dashboard.
    """
    if request.method == 'POST':
        username = request.form.get('username')
        email = request.form.get('email')
        country = request.form.get('country')
        password = request.form.get('password')
        confirm_password = request.form.get('confirm_password')

        if not username or not email or not country or not password or not confirm_password:
            return render_template('signup.html', error='All fields are required')
        if password != confirm_password:
            return render_template('signup.html', error='Passwords do not match')

        # Weak encryption of password (DO NOT USE IN PRODUCTION)
        hashed_password = custom_hash(password)

        try:
            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                query = "INSERT INTO users (username, email, country, password) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)"
                cursor.execute(query, (username, email, country, hashed_password))
                conn.commit()
        except Exception as e:
            print("Error inserting user:", e)  # Print the error message for debugging
            return render_template('signup.html', error='User already exists or database error')

        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (username,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()

        session['username'] = user['username']
        session['user_id'] = user['id']

        # Update last_login timestamp
        cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET last_login = ? WHERE username = ?", (datetime.datetime.now(), username))
        conn.commit()
        conn.close()

        return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))

    return render_template('signup.html')

def custom_hash(password):
    """
    Weak password hashing function using MD5.

    Args:
    - password: The password to hash.

    Returns:
    - The hashed password.
    """
    return hashlib.md5(password.encode()).hexdigest()

def signup():
    """
    Handles user registration.

    On GET request:
    Renders the signup page.

    On POST request:
    Retrieves user registration data from the signup form.
    Performs basic validation checks on the input data.
    Checks if the provided username already exists in the database.
    Inserts the new user into the database.
    Stores the username in the session upon successful registration and redirects to the dashboard.
    """

        username = request.form.get('username')
        email = request.form.get('email')
        country = request.form.get('country')
        password = request.form.get('password')
        confirm_password = request.form.get('confirm_password')

        if not username or not email or not country or not password or not confirm_password:
            return render_template('signup.html', error='All fields are required')
        if password != confirm_password:
            return render_template('signup.html', error='Passwords do not match')

        # Weak encryption of password (DO NOT USE IN PRODUCTION)
        hashed_password = custom_hash(password)

        try:
            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()

                cursor.execute(query, (username, email, country, hashed_password))
                conn.commit()
        except Exception as e:
            print("Error inserting user:", e)  # Print the error message for debugging
            return render_template('signup.html', error='User already exists or database error')

        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (username,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()

        session['username'] = user['username']
        session['user_id'] = user['id']

        # Update last_login timestamp
        cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET last_login = ? WHERE username = ?", (datetime.datetime.now(), username))
        conn.commit()
        conn.close()

        return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))

    return render_template('signup.html')

server/routes.py:66

def signup():
    """
    Handles user registration.

    On GET request:
    Renders the signup page.

    On POST request:
    Retrieves user registration data from the signup form.
    Performs basic validation checks on the input data.
    Checks if the provided username already exists in the database.
    Inserts the new user into the database.
    Stores the username in the session upon successful registration and redirects to the dashboard.
    """
    if request.method == 'POST':
        username = request.form.get('username')
        email = request.form.get('email')
        country = request.form.get('country')
        password = request.form.get('password')
        confirm_password = request.form.get('confirm_password')

        if not username or not email or not country or not password or not confirm_password:
            return render_template('signup.html', error='All fields are required')
        if password != confirm_password:
            return render_template('signup.html', error='Passwords do not match')

        # Weak encryption of password (DO NOT USE IN PRODUCTION)
        hashed_password = custom_hash(password)

        try:
            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                query = "INSERT INTO users (username, email, country, password) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)"
                cursor.execute(query, (username, email, country, hashed_password))
                conn.commit()
        except Exception as e:
            print("Error inserting user:", e)  # Print the error message for debugging
            return render_template('signup.html', error='User already exists or database error')

        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (username,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()

        session['username'] = user['username']
        session['user_id'] = user['id']

        # Update last_login timestamp
        cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET last_login = ? WHERE username = ?", (datetime.datetime.now(), username))
        conn.commit()
        conn.close()

        return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))

    return render_template('signup.html')

server/routes.py:22

def login():
    """
    Handles user login.

    On GET request:
    Renders the login page.

    On POST request:
    Retrieves the username and password from the login form.
    Queries the database for the user based on the provided username.
    Checks if the provided password matches the hashed password stored in the database.
    If authentication succeeds, stores the username in the session and redirects to the dashboard.
    If authentication fails, renders the login page with an error message.
    """
    if request.method == 'POST':
        username = request.form.get('username')
        password = request.form.get('password')
        if not username or not password:
            return render_template('login.html', error='Please provide username and password')

        # Query the database for the user using parameterized query
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ? OR email = ?"
            cursor.execute(query, (username,username))
            user = cursor.fetchone()

        # Check if the user exists and the password matches
        if user and user['password'] == custom_hash(password):
            session['username'] = user['username']
            session['user_id'] = user['id']

            # Update last_login timestamp
            cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET last_login = ? WHERE username = ?", (datetime.datetime.now(), username))
            conn.commit()
            conn.close()

            return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))
        else:
            return render_template('login.html', error='Invalid username or password')
    return render_template('login.html')

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.

server/routes.py:22

def login():
    """
    Handles user login.

    On GET request:
    Renders the login page.

    On POST request:
    Retrieves the username and password from the login form.
    Queries the database for the user based on the provided username.
    Checks if the provided password matches the hashed password stored in the database.
    If authentication succeeds, stores the username in the session and redirects to the dashboard.
    If authentication fails, renders the login page with an error message.
    """
    if request.method == 'POST':
        username = request.form.get('username')
        password = request.form.get('password')
        if not username or not password:
            return render_template('login.html', error='Please provide username and password')

        # Query the database for the user using parameterized query
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ? OR email = ?"
            cursor.execute(query, (username,username))
            user = cursor.fetchone()

        # Check if the user exists and the password matches
        if user and user['password'] == custom_hash(password):
            session['username'] = user['username']
            session['user_id'] = user['id']

            # Update last_login timestamp
            cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET last_login = ? WHERE username = ?", (datetime.datetime.now(), username))
            conn.commit()
            conn.close()

            return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))
        else:
            return render_template('login.html', error='Invalid username or password')
    return render_template('login.html')

def custom_hash(password):
    """
    Weak password hashing function using MD5.

    Args:
    - password: The password to hash.

    Returns:
    - The hashed password.
    """
    return hashlib.md5(password.encode()).hexdigest()

server/routes.py:249

def upload_file():
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    if request.method == 'POST':
        if 'file' not in request.files:
            flash('No file part', 'error')
            return redirect(request.url)
        file = request.files['file']
        if file.filename == '':
            flash('No selected file', 'error')
            return redirect(request.url)
        if file:
            filename = file.filename
            filepath = os.path.join(UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
            file.save(filepath)

            # Get user_id from the session username
            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
                cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
                user = cursor.fetchone()
                user_id = user['id']

            # Insert the file into the files table
            cursor.execute("INSERT INTO files (filename, user_id) VALUES (?, ?)", (filename, user_id))
            conn.commit()

            flash('File successfully uploaded', 'success')
            return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))
    return render_template('upload.html')

def search():
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    query = request.args.get('query')
    results = []

    # Simulate a search operation (this could be a database query in a real application)
    # For demonstration purposes, just echo the query back as a result
    if query:
        results.append(query)

    return render_template('dashboard.html', results=results, query=query)

server/routes.py:157

def edit_profile():
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    # Get user_id from the session username
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
        user = cursor.fetchone()

    return render_template('edit_profile.html', user=user)

server/routes.py:157

def edit_profile():
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    # Get user_id from the session username
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
        user = cursor.fetchone()

    return render_template('edit_profile.html', user=user)

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

);

server/routes.py:341

def delete_file(filename):
    file_path = os.path.join(UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
    if 'username' not in session:
        return jsonify({'message': 'You are not logged in.', 'type': 'error'})

    # Get user_id from the session username
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
        user_id = user['id']

    # Check if the file belongs to the user
    query = "SELECT * FROM files WHERE filename = ? AND user_id = ?"
    cursor.execute(query, (filename, user_id))
    file = cursor.fetchone()

    if not file:
        return jsonify({'message': 'File not found.', 'type': 'error'})

    # Remove the file from the filesystem and database
    if os.path.exists(file_path):
        os.remove(file_path)
        cursor.execute("DELETE FROM files WHERE filename = ? AND user_id = ?", (filename, user_id))
        conn.commit()
        return jsonify({'message': 'File deleted successfully.', 'type': 'success'})
    else:
        return jsonify({'message': 'File not found on server.', 'type': 'error'})

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

server/api/v1/api_v1.py:223

def delete(self, user_id):
        """
            Delete a user from the application.
        """
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            cursor.execute("SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?", (user_id,))
            user = cursor.fetchone()
            if user:
                cursor.execute("DELETE FROM users WHERE id = ?", (user_id,))
                conn.commit()
                response = make_response(jsonify({'message': 'User deleted successfully'}), 200)
                return response
            else:
                response = make_response(jsonify({'error': 'User not found'}), 404)
                return response

server/api/v1/api_v1.py:110

def post(self):
        """
        Handles user login.
        """
        data = request.get_json()  # Get data from request body
        username = data.get('username')
        password = data.get('password')

        if not username or not password:
            return {'error': 'Please provide username and password'}, 400

        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ? OR email = ?"
            cursor.execute(query, (username, username))
            user = cursor.fetchone()

        if user and user['password'] == custom_hash(password):

            # Generate JWT
            token = jwt.encode({
                'username': username,
                'exp': datetime.datetime.utcnow() + datetime.timedelta(hours=24)  # Token expires in 24 hours
            }, Config.SECRET_KEY, algorithm='HS256')


            # Update last_login timestamp
            with get_db_connection() as conn:
                cursor = conn.cursor()
                cursor.execute("UPDATE users SET last_login = ? WHERE username = ?", (datetime.datetime.now(), username))
                conn.commit()

            return {'message': 'Login successful', 'token': token}, 200
        else:
            return {'error': 'Invalid username or password'}, 401

def custom_hash(password):
    """
    Weak password hashing function using MD5.

    Args:
    - password: The password to hash.

    Returns:
    - The hashed password.
    """
    return hashlib.md5(password.encode()).hexdigest()

server/routes.py:313

def download_file(filename):
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    username = session['username']

    # Query the database for the user ID
    # Get user_id from the session username
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (username,))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
        user_id = user['id']

    # Check if the file exists and belongs to the user
    query = "SELECT * FROM files WHERE filename = ? AND user_id = ?"
    cursor.execute(query, (filename, user_id))
    file = cursor.fetchone()

    if file:
        return send_from_directory(UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
    else:
        flash('You do not have permission to download this file.', 'error')
        return redirect(url_for('dashboard'))

server/routes.py:171

def update_profile():
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    username = session['username']
    # Get current user info
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (username,))
        current_user = cursor.fetchone()

    # Get form data
    new_username = request.form['username'] or current_user['username']
    email = request.form['email'] or current_user['email']
    country = request.form['country'] or current_user['country']
    if 'role' not in request.form:
        role = current_user['role']
    else:
        role = request.form['role'] or current_user['role']
    #role = request.form['role'] or current_user['role']
    #permissions = request.form['permissions'] or current_user['permissions']
    if 'permissions' not in request.form:
        permissions = current_user['permissions']
    else:
        permissions = request.form['permissions']
    team = request.form['team'] or current_user['team']

    # Handle profile picture upload
    profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']
    if 'profile_picture' in request.files:
        file = request.files['profile_picture']
        if file:
            # Generate simple incremental ID for profile picture
            # Get the highest existing ID and increment it by 1
            cursor.execute("SELECT MAX(profile_picture) FROM users")
            max_id = cursor.fetchone()[0]
            if max_id is None:
                max_id = 0
            if max_id:
                profile_picture_id = max_id + 1
            else:
                profile_picture_id = 1

            # Save profile picture with simple ID
            filename = str(profile_picture_id) + '.png'
            file.save(os.path.join(PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename))

            #filename = file.filename
            #file.save(os.path.join(PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename))
            #profile_picture = filename
        else:
            profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']

    # Update the user in the database
    try:
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = """
                UPDATE users
                SET username = ?, email = ?, country = ?, role = ?, permissions = ?, team = ?, profile_picture = ?
                WHERE username = ?
            """
            cursor.execute(query, (new_username, email, country, role, permissions, team, profile_picture_id, username))
            conn.commit()

        session['username'] = new_username  # Update session username if changed
        flash('Profile updated successfully.', 'success')
        return redirect(url_for('profile'))
    except conn.Error as e:
        print("SQLite error:", e)
        # Rollback the transaction
        conn.rollback()
        flash('Fail to update profile.', 'error')
        return redirect(url_for('edit_profile'))

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

server/routes.py:171

def update_profile():
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    username = session['username']
    # Get current user info
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT * FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (username,))
        current_user = cursor.fetchone()

    # Get form data
    new_username = request.form['username'] or current_user['username']
    email = request.form['email'] or current_user['email']
    country = request.form['country'] or current_user['country']
    if 'role' not in request.form:
        role = current_user['role']
    else:
        role = request.form['role'] or current_user['role']
    #role = request.form['role'] or current_user['role']
    #permissions = request.form['permissions'] or current_user['permissions']
    if 'permissions' not in request.form:
        permissions = current_user['permissions']
    else:
        permissions = request.form['permissions']
    team = request.form['team'] or current_user['team']

    # Handle profile picture upload
    profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']
    if 'profile_picture' in request.files:
        file = request.files['profile_picture']
        if file:
            # Generate simple incremental ID for profile picture
            # Get the highest existing ID and increment it by 1
            cursor.execute("SELECT MAX(profile_picture) FROM users")
            max_id = cursor.fetchone()[0]
            if max_id is None:
                max_id = 0
            if max_id:
                profile_picture_id = max_id + 1
            else:
                profile_picture_id = 1

            # Save profile picture with simple ID
            filename = str(profile_picture_id) + '.png'
            file.save(os.path.join(PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename))

            #filename = file.filename
            #file.save(os.path.join(PROFILE_PICTURES_UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename))
            #profile_picture = filename
        else:
            profile_picture_id = current_user['profile_picture']

    # Update the user in the database
    try:
        with get_db_connection() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()
            query = """
                UPDATE users
                SET username = ?, email = ?, country = ?, role = ?, permissions = ?, team = ?, profile_picture = ?
                WHERE username = ?
            """
            cursor.execute(query, (new_username, email, country, role, permissions, team, profile_picture_id, username))
            conn.commit()

        session['username'] = new_username  # Update session username if changed
        flash('Profile updated successfully.', 'success')
        return redirect(url_for('profile'))
    except conn.Error as e:
        print("SQLite error:", e)
        # Rollback the transaction
        conn.rollback()
        flash('Fail to update profile.', 'error')
        return redirect(url_for('edit_profile'))

def get_db_connection():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DATABASE_URI)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row  # This allows name-based access to columns
    return conn

server/routes.py:284

def uploaded_file(filename):
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    # Get user_id from the session username
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
        user_id = user['id']

    # Check if the file belongs to the user
    query = "SELECT * FROM files WHERE filename = ? AND user_id = ?"
    cursor.execute(query, (filename, user_id))
    file = cursor.fetchone()

    if file:
        return send_from_directory(UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
    else:
        return "Forbidden", 403

Investigation and possible remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Pass untrusted values through the verified driver's parameter-binding API. Keep SQL query, update, and insert structure separate from data. Review NoSQL APIs separately.
  • Restrict each field as far as its business requirement allows. A string or length check is not injection protection. A comment or other free-text field may contain any character, so the sink construction is the injection control.
  • Add regression cases for normal input, rejected malformed input and query-structure preservation; resolve inconclusive guard and binding evidence before applying a fix.

server/routes.py:284

def uploaded_file(filename):
    if 'username' not in session:
        return redirect(url_for('login'))

    # Get user_id from the session username
    with get_db_connection() as conn:
        cursor = conn.cursor()
        query = "SELECT id FROM users WHERE username = ?"
        cursor.execute(query, (session['username'],))
        user = cursor.fetchone()
        user_id = user['id']

    # Check if the file belongs to the user
    query = "SELECT * FROM files WHERE filename = ? AND user_id = ?"
    cursor.execute(query, (filename, user_id))
    file = cursor.fetchone()

    if file:
        return send_from_directory(UPLOAD_FOLDER, filename)
    else:
        return "Forbidden", 403

def get_db_connection():
    conn = sqlite3.connect(DATABASE_URI)
    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row  # This allows name-based access to columns
    return conn

Remediation

  • Remediate established vulnerabilities and poor practice. For Review items, resolve the named evidence before deciding whether or how behavior needs to change.
  • Use escaped template output for HTML text and quoted non-active attributes; use textContent for plain DOM text.
  • For intentional rich HTML, apply a verified maintained sanitizer before every sink; inspect its configuration and version.
  • Treat script, URL, event-handler and unquoted-attribute contexts separately. HTML entity encoding alone does not protect them.
  • Preserve source, storage and output links when fixing reflected or stored flows; test the protected counterpart.

Scope & limitations

  • ESM relative imports, literal CommonJS requires and direct exported handlers are resolved; dynamic imports, re-exports and mutated module bindings remain uncertain
  • Verified Express receiver construction and direct registrations required; chained routes, mount composition and effective middleware are not resolved
  • Supported helper calls bind identifier parameters and direct returns; branches, closures, wrappers, unknown calls, recursion, and asynchronous response continuations remain uncertain
  • SQL text, NoSQL $where strings, query documents, and request-field validation are candidate findings, not confirmed vulnerabilities
  • Python adapter v1: static Flask/Blueprint, RESTX Resource and FastAPI routes; dynamic registration, custom decorators and response overrides remain gaps
  • Python helper expansion is bounded to depth 3 and 24 calls per route; loops, comprehensions, mutation, exceptions and external dispatch are conservative
  • Jinja output requires a verified Flask template binding; unresolved configuration, includes/macros and stored origins remain Review; no runtime execution or SCA

Finding-type glossary · 11 definitions

Types identify security review areas. A type name does not establish vulnerability or detector coverage; always read the result status.

Client-side XSS

Coverage: bounded browser DOM and Angular/React/Vue analysis

Browser or API data reaches a DOM or framework rendering operation in an active output context.

How to interpret this type

Client entries identify browser sources and verified sinks. Raw flows are construction findings; unknown origin, trust-to-template linkage or API response mapping remains Review. Framework defaults can protect a specific output.

Remediation approach

Prefer text APIs and default framework escaping/sanitization. Sanitize rich HTML before rendering, remove unnecessary trust bypasses and verify API origins.

Rendering Flow Review

Coverage: bounded API response evidence

Data returned by an API needs its browser consumer linked before a rendering claim can be assessed.

How to interpret this type

JSON output alone is not an XSS sink or a vulnerability. This record is incomplete flow context for further analysis.

Remediation approach

Locate the browser consumer, establish its output context and protections, and assess that rendering flow before changing API data handling.

SQL Injection

Coverage: bounded analysis

Input changes executable SQL structure instead of remaining a data value, potentially changing what the database reads or writes.

How to interpret this type

An SQL Injection pill identifies the risk family being reviewed. String-built executable SQL is poor practice to remediate even without a proven exploit; a library-managed search value may remain unresolved. The finding status and evidence determine the actual claim. The pill alone does not confirm injection.

Remediation approach

Use the verified driver's parameter-binding API for values. Review dynamic identifiers separately. Keep business-field validation independent of query-structure protection.

NoSQL Injection

Coverage: bounded analysis

Untrusted input changes a NoSQL query's operators, selector structure, or executable expression rather than remaining an allowed data value.

How to interpret this type

Executable $where string construction is poor practice to refactor even without a proven exploit. An unmodeled document constraint alone is Review, not an established defect. Inspect actual value types, keys, transformations, library behavior, and the named status.

Remediation approach

Prefer data-only query APIs, constrain scalar slots and permitted keys/operators, and avoid building executable $where strings from input.

Input Validation

Coverage: bounded analysis

Server-side checks enforce the business requirements for a field, such as required presence, runtime type, permitted format, range, or length.

How to interpret this type

Poor input validation means an applicable requirement is not adequately enforced. This neutral family label also covers unresolved checks and supported protection: no validator identified in an analyzed slice is not proof that every execution path lacks validation. Free-text punctuation is not itself a defect.

Remediation approach

Define field-specific requirements and enforce them before use on all applicable paths. Verify guard behavior and middleware. Apply destination-specific protection separately.

Account Creation Validation

Coverage: bounded analysis

Required account fields and enforcing guards must be checked before an account is persisted.

How to interpret this type

Review field requirements, conditional bypasses, and generated persistence behavior. A modeled create operation alone does not prove a registration bypass.

Remediation approach

Enforce required fields before persistence, terminate rejected paths, add appropriate database constraints, and test the persisted result.

Outbound Request Safety

Coverage: bounded analysis

Input used in an outbound request may affect its destination, headers, or other request values. Unrestricted destination control can lead to server-side request forgery (SSRF).

How to interpret this type

The current scanner records bounded outbound-use evidence. A pill does not establish SSRF, destination reachability, or complete response-flow analysis.

Remediation approach

Verify allowed destinations and protocols, redirect handling, network boundaries, and sensitive request values for the specific HTTP client.

Reflected XSS

Coverage: bounded server response and template analysis

Request-controlled content is reflected into a browser response in a context that allows it to execute as script.

How to interpret this type

Request input reaches an HTML response or template output. Raw output is a construction finding; unknown context or sanitizers remain Review. Escaping protects only its modeled output context. Runtime exploitation is not implied.

Remediation approach

Use context-appropriate output encoding and safe template or DOM APIs. Input validation alone does not replace correct output handling.

Stored XSS

Coverage: bounded model-field write/read and server template analysis

Untrusted data is persisted and later rendered in an active browser context.

How to interpret this type

A matching model and field link source writes to rendering reads. Unresolved write origins remain Review; a verified raw path is a construction finding. A dependency version alone does not establish a reachable XSS flow.

Remediation approach

Preserve the intended stored data; apply context-appropriate escaping or maintained sanitization at every rendering sink, and review existing stored content.

Sensitive Data Logging

Coverage: bounded analysis

Logs expose credentials, tokens, personal information, or other sensitive values to readers or storage that should not receive them.

How to interpret this type

Covers verified console and winston calls in analyzed route handlers that receive request values with credential or personal-data evidence. A field name is a hint for review, not proof; Authorization and Cookie headers are credentials by HTTP semantics. Morgan request logging that records URLs is checked against credentials read from the query string. Constant messages are not leaks. Non-request secrets and logger redaction configuration are not yet analyzed.

Remediation approach

Avoid logging secrets; minimize sensitive fields and apply verified redaction before emission. Review log access and retention.

Sensitive Operation Review

Coverage: bounded analysis

A possible sensitive use was recorded but does not yet map to a more specific supported risk family.

How to interpret this type

This is an analysis category. Inspect the evidence and missing operation semantics before making a security claim.

Remediation approach

Resolve the source, operation, API behavior, and required protection before selecting a fix.
